Initial curated pass. Book initial curated pass complete. Overall next: 1 Samuel 19:1. Greek-based Saints-Translation numbering is retained.

Completed means the initial curated cross-reference pass: the source verse and every listed target passage were read in the pinned Saints-Translation English records, and each retained pair has a specific explanation. It does not mean every possible biblical connection has been exhausted, or independent scholarly approval.

Joshua 1:1

After the death of Moses, the Lord spoke to Joshua the son of Nun, Moses’ attendant, saying:

  1. Deuteronomy 34:5-9 Moses' death followed by Joshua's succession

    Joshua's opening after Moses' death follows the conclusion recording that death and Joshua's reception of understanding and Israel's obedience; the two records retain their different introductory details.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 34:5 And Moses, the household servant of the Lord, died in the land of Moab by the word of the Lord.

    Deuteronomy 34:6 And they buried him in Gai, in the land of Moab, near the house of Peor; and no man knows his burial place unto this day.

    Deuteronomy 34:7 And Moses was a hundred and twenty years old when he died; his eyes were not dimmed, neither were his cheeks wasted away.

    Deuteronomy 34:8 And the sons of Israel wept for Moses in Araboth of Moab, at the Jordan over against Jericho, thirty days; and the days of mourning and weeping for Moses were ended.

    Deuteronomy 34:9 And Joshua the son of Nun was filled with a spirit of understanding; for Moses had laid his hands upon him; and the sons of Israel hearkened unto him, and did as the Lord commanded Moses.

    Pinned source record

  2. Exodus 33:11 Joshua son of Nun as Moses' attendant

    Both explicitly identify Joshua son of Nun as Moses' attendant; Exodus describes his remaining at the tent, while Joshua opens with the Lord addressing him after Moses' death.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 33:11 The Lord spoke to Moses face to face, as a man speaks to his friend. Then Moses returned to the camp, but his young attendant Joshua son of Nun did not leave the tent.

    Pinned source record

Joshua 1:2

“Moses My servant has died. Now therefore arise and cross the Jordan, you and all this people, into the land that I am giving them.

  1. Deuteronomy 31:3 Promised leadership into possession and the command to cross

    Moses announces Joshua going before Israel into possession; the later divine charge directs Joshua and the people to cross Jordan into the land being given them.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 31:3 The Lord your God, who goes before your face, shall Himself destroy these nations from before your face, and you shall take possession of them; and Joshua shall go before your face, as the Lord has spoken.

    Pinned source record

Joshua 1:3

Every place where you tread with the sole of your feet I will give you, as I have spoken to Moses.

  1. Deuteronomy 11:24 Repeated footsole-and-land promise

    Both promise possession of the places trodden by the soles of Israel's feet; Joshua's charge expressly relates the gift to what was spoken to Moses.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 11:24 Every place whereon the sole of your foot shall tread shall be yours. From the wilderness and Anti-Lebanon, and from the great river, the river Euphrates, even unto the western sea, shall be your borders.

    Pinned source record

Joshua 1:4

The wilderness and Anti-Lebanon, as far as the great river, the River Euphrates, and as far as the furthest sea toward the setting sun shall be your borders.

  1. Deuteronomy 11:24 Corresponding territorial boundary declarations

    Both name wilderness, Anti-Lebanon, the Euphrates and the western or sunset sea as boundaries, preserving the source's actual geographical expressions without modern boundary verification.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 11:24 Every place whereon the sole of your foot shall tread shall be yours. From the wilderness and Anti-Lebanon, and from the great river, the river Euphrates, even unto the western sea, shall be your borders.

    Pinned source record

Joshua 1:5

No man shall withstand you all the days of your life. As I was with Moses, so I will be with you; I will not forsake you or disregard you.

  1. Deuteronomy 31:8 Non-abandonment assurance to Joshua

    Moses assures Joshua that the Lord will not fail or forsake him; the later divine speech similarly promises not to forsake or disregard him, distinguishing the speakers.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 31:8 And the Lord who goes with you will not fail you, neither will He forsake you; fear not, neither be fainthearted.

Joshua 1:6

Be strong and courageous, for you shall apportion to this people the land which I swore to your fathers to give them.

  1. Deuteronomy 31:7 Joshua's courage and allotment of the sworn land

    Both address Joshua with strength and courage because he will lead Israel into the land sworn to the fathers and give it as inheritance.

    Read matching passage

Joshua 1:7

Be strong and courageous, then, to keep and do what Moses My servant commanded you. Do not turn aside from these commands to the right or to the left, so that you may have understanding in all that you do.

  1. Deuteronomy 5:32-33 Obedience without turning right or left

    Both command fidelity to the delivered commands without turning right or left; Joshua's personal charge also stresses strength and understanding, while Deuteronomy addresses Israel's life in the land.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 5:32 And you shall take heed to do as the Lord your God commanded you; you shall turn neither to the right nor to the left,

Joshua 1:8

This book of the law shall not depart from your mouth. Meditate on it day and night, so that you may understand how to do all that is written. Then you shall prosper and make your ways prosper, and then you shall understand.

  1. Psalms 1:2-3 Day-and-night meditation on the law and resulting prosperity

    Both join meditation on the Lord's law day and night with prospering conduct; the psalm uses a fruitful tree image, while Joshua's charge stresses doing everything written.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 1:2 but his will is in the Lord’s law, and on His law he will meditate day and night.

    Psalms 1:3 He will be like the tree planted beside the outlets of waters, which will give its fruit in its season, and whose leaf will not fall off; and everything he does will be prospered.

    Pinned source record

  2. Deuteronomy 17:18-19 Written law kept continually by a leader

    Joshua must keep the law in his mouth and meditate continually; the royal instruction likewise requires retaining and reading a law scroll, preserving the distinct offices and descriptions.

Joshua 1:9

Behold, I have commanded you: be strong and courageous. Do not be fainthearted or afraid, for the Lord your God is with you wherever you go.”

  1. Deuteronomy 31:6 Courage grounded in the Lord's presence

    Both forbid fear and faintheartedness and call for strength and courage because the Lord is present; Deuteronomy addresses Israel collectively, while Joshua receives the later personal charge.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 31:6 Be courageous and strong; fear not, neither be fainthearted, neither be terrified before their face; for the Lord your God, who goes before you, is with you and among you. He will in no wise fail you, neither will He forsake you.

    Deuteronomy 1:24 And they turned and went up into the hill country, and came unto the Valley of the Cluster, and searched it out.

Joshua 2:10

For we have heard that the Lord God dried up the Red Sea before you when you came out of the land of Egypt, and all that He did to the two kings of the Amorites beyond the Jordan, Sihon and Og, whom you destroyed.

  1. Exodus 14:21-29 Rahab's Red Sea report and the narrated dry crossing

    Rahab recalls the Lord drying the Red Sea before Israel; Exodus narrates the sea made dry and Israel crossing, while also recording the Egyptian pursuit and destruction.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 14:21 Moses stretched out his hand over the sea. The Lord drove the sea back with a violent south wind all night and made the sea dry. The water was split.

    Exodus 14:22 The sons of Israel entered the midst of the sea on dry ground, and the water was a wall for them on the right and a wall on the left.

    Exodus 14:23 The Egyptians pursued them, and all Pharaoh’s horses, the chariots, and the riders entered after them into the midst of the sea.

    Exodus 14:24 During the morning watch, the Lord looked on the Egyptians’ camp in a pillar of fire and cloud and threw the Egyptians’ camp into confusion.

    Exodus 14:25 He bound the axles of their chariots and drove them with force. The Egyptians said, “Let us flee from Israel, for the Lord is fighting for them against the Egyptians.”

    Exodus 14:26 And the Lord said to Moses, “Stretch out your hand over the sea, and let the water return and cover the Egyptians, both their chariots and their riders.”

    Exodus 14:27 Moses stretched out his hand over the sea, and toward daybreak the water returned to its place. The Egyptians fled beneath the water, and the Lord shook the Egyptians into the midst of the sea.

    Exodus 14:28 The water returned and covered the chariots, the riders, and all Pharaoh’s force that had entered the sea after them. Not one of them remained.

    Exodus 14:29 But the sons of Israel walked on dry ground in the midst of the sea, and the water was a wall for them on the right and a wall on the left.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 21:23 And Sihon would not allow Israel to pass through his territory. He gathered all his people and went out into the wilderness to array himself against Israel. He came to Jahaz and arrayed himself against Israel.

    Numbers 21:24 And Israel struck him by slaughter with the sword, and they took control of his land from the Arnon to the Jabbok, as far as the sons of Ammon, because Jazer is the border of the sons of Ammon.

    Numbers 21:25 And Israel took all these towns and settled in all the Amorite towns, in Heshbon and all the towns adjoining it.

    Both place Joshua's burial in his inheritance in Ephraim's mountain north of Gaash; Thamnathasachara and Timnath-heres remain in their supplied forms without independent name identification.

Joshua 24:31a

There they placed with him in the tomb in which they buried him the stone knives with which he circumcised the sons of Israel at Gilgal, when he brought them out of Egypt, as the Lord commanded them. They are there to this very day.

  1. Joshua 5:2-3 Buried circumcision knives and their earlier commanded making

    The continuation places the stone circumcision knives in Joshua's tomb; the earlier narrative records the command to make sharp stone knives and Joshua making and using them.

Joshua 24:32

The sons of Israel brought Joseph’s bones up from Egypt and buried them at Shechem in the portion of the field that Jacob acquired from the Amorites living at Shechem for one hundred ewe lambs; he gave it to Joseph as a portion.

  1. Exodus 13:19 Joseph's bones carried from Egypt and finally buried

    Joshua records the bones brought up from Egypt and buried at Shechem; Exodus expressly describes Moses taking those bones in fulfillment of Joseph's oath instruction.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 13:19 Moses took Joseph’s bones with him, for Joseph had made the sons of Israel swear an oath, saying, “The Lord will surely visit you, and you shall carry my bones up from here with you.”

Joshua 24:33

After these things Eleazar son of Aaron, the high priest, died and was buried at Gabaath of Phinehas his son, which he had given him in the mountain of Ephraim.

  1. Joshua 24:33a Same-chapter Eleazar's death and Phinehas' subsequent priestly service

    Eleazar dies and is buried at his son's Gabaath; the continuation records Phinehas succeeding him and eventually being buried at his own Gabaath, preserving both notices.

Joshua 24:33a

On that day the sons of Israel took the ark of God and carried it about among themselves. Phinehas served as priest in place of Eleazar his father until he died and was buried at his own Gabaath.

  1. Numbers 25:11-13 Phinehas' priestly line and covenant before his succession

    The continuation names Phinehas succeeding Eleazar; Numbers identifies him as Eleazar's son in Aaron's line and grants him and his seed an enduring priesthood covenant.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 25:11 Phinehas son of Eleazar, son of Aaron the priest, has stopped My wrath against the sons of Israel by being zealous with My zeal among them, and I did not consume the sons of Israel in My zeal.

    Numbers 25:12 Therefore say: Behold, I give him a covenant of peace.

    Numbers 25:13 And it shall be for him and his seed after him an everlasting covenant of priesthood, because he was zealous for his God and made atonement for the sons of Israel.

Joshua 24:33b

The sons of Israel went away, each to his own place and his own city. The sons of Israel revered Astarte and Ashtaroth and the gods of the nations around them. The Lord handed them into the hands of Eglom king of Moab, and he ruled over them for eighteen years.

  1. Judges 2:13-14 Other-god service followed by delivery to enemies

    Both connect serving other gods with the Lord handing Israel to enemies; Joshua names Astarte, Ashtaroth and Eglom, while Judges names Baal and Astartes with a broader enemy description.

    Judges 2:13 They forsook the Lord and served Baal and the Astartes.

    Judges 2:14 The Lord was greatly angered against Israel. He delivered them into the hand of plunderers, who plundered them; and He sold them into the hand of their enemies all around, so they could not stand before their enemies.
